  NOTE: rows found in metadata pages (in their free space, or still listed on an old page), not the volume's
        current content. FOUND IN is the directory whose page held the row (its path today), not necessarily
        where the file was deleted from. One line stands for every copy of a row.
  -v: each entry's locator, both times, the copies found, and why it cannot be carved
  To recover: `export deleted DIR --carve` carves the 34 whose extent map survives (best effort).
── Recovery methods ──
  Mode: recovery.  Ran: Trash table (0xd), checkpoint diff, B+-tree node-slack scan (live pages
        only), orphaned-page scan.
  This is `recovery`: live-page slack only (the pages the tree walk reads and, when an entry's file is
  live, every live file's extent map once). Run `deleted --full` to ALSO scan orphan pages (the freed
  pages of deleted objects) -- that reads the WHOLE volume once (about a minute per 60-100 GiB), but
  it typically finds far more; `export deleted --full DIR` also carves extent-backed content.
  Fine-grained: --no-slack · --scan-pages · --orphans · --search SUB · --csv/--json/--jsonl FILE ·
                `export deleted DIR` (with --carve).
  --max-scan N stops the orphan scan early (faster, but the run is then INCOMPLETE); `help deleted`
  lists what each option costs.
  Also: `deleted --orphans` adds a low-confidence tier for OID-Table objects unlinked from the tree.
  Stream snapshots of files that still exist: `snapshots`.  Windows Recycle Bin: `recyclebin`.
